DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player taps to unleash an expanding shockwave that pushes nearby obstacles outward, briefly clearing a corridor for the dot to advance. The satisfaction comes from instantly reshaping the field and timing bursts to forge a path.

## Player
The player controls a small glowing dot that drifts forward. Visually it is a tiny neon sphere. Its verbs are:
- **tap** – emit a shockwave centered on the dot.
- **move** – the dot drifts forward automatically (or can be nudged).

## Core loop
Every 1–3 seconds the dot approaches a cluster of obstacles. The player watches the incoming hazards, decides when to tap, and watches the shockwave push obstacles aside, creating a fleeting gap. The dot then moves through the gap, the wave dissipates, and the cycle repeats, rewarding timing and anticipation.

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Sliding blocks** that glide horizontally or vertically, colliding with the dot if not displaced.
- **Rotating spinners** that spin in place and extend outward, threatening the dot’s path.
- **Static walls** that sit in the arena; when struck by a shockwave they are pushed temporarily before resettling.
- **Bursting mines** that remain dormant until the shockwave reaches them, then explode outward, forcing the player to time bursts carefully.

All obstacles are pushed outward by the shockwave for a brief moment, then resume their original motion.

## Score
- Each time the dot successfully passes through a cleared corridor.
- Each obstacle displaced by a shockwave without colliding with the dot.
- Bonus points for chaining multiple taps without being hit.

## Win and lose
- **Lose**: An obstacle contacts the dot; the game shows “Game Over” and the final score.
- **Win**: The dot reaches a predefined endpoint (e.g., the far side of the arena) or survives a set number of obstacle waves. The win screen celebrates with a burst of neon and the final score.

## Feel
Frantic, neon‑lit arcade action with a pulse‑driven rhythm. The visual style is high‑contrast neon on a dark background, complemented by crisp arcade‑style blips and a low‑frequency hum. The experience feels like a rapid, reactive dance of light and motion.

## Why it's fun
The thrill of timing a single tap to blast obstacles aside, watching the shockwave ripple and the dot surge forward in the newly cleared path.

TASKS.md

show
1. HTML scaffold + meta.json — add required header, canvas, restart button, empty script block and matching meta.json.
2. Game state objects — define plain objects for player, hazards, score, and phase at top of script with initial values.
3. requestAnimationFrame loop — implement startLoop with cancelAnimationFrame, loop body with try/catch, update(dt) and render() calls, clear canvas.
4. Input handling — set up a keys Set with keydown/keyup listeners, R key restart and restart button trigger reset().
5. Player movement — implement dt‑based physics for player based on key inputs; ensure controls affect player state.
6. Hazards — create hazard data structure, spawning respecting SAFE_RADIUS, and motion scaled by dt.
7. Collision detection — detect player‑hazard collisions, transition state to "gameover" and stop gameplay.
8. Score — increment score on discrete events and draw it at the top‑left of the canvas.
9. Render — clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, and HUD; keep player high‑contrast.
10. Game‑over overlay — dim background, display "Game Over", final score, and restart instructions.
11. Observable contract — expose window.__game with state, score, player position/visibility, and objective each frame.
12. Audio — lazily initialise AudioContext, provide beep() helper, and hook sounds to hit, score, and death events.

<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Force Burst</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Tap to unleash a shockwave that pushes nearby obstacles outward, clearing a temporary corridor for the dot to advance.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Tap to emit a shockwave</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
    // === CONFIG =============================================================
// (none for now)

// === STATE ==============================================================
const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

// Game state objects.
let player = {
  x: canvas.width / 2,
  y: canvas.height / 2,
  radius: 10,
  visible: true,
};

let hazards = []; // will hold obstacle objects

let score = 0;
let phase = "menu"; // "menu" | "playing" | "gameover"

    // === RESET ==============================================================
    function reset() {
      // Reset core state.
      player.x = canvas.width / 2;
      player.y = canvas.height / 2;
      player.visible = true;
      hazards = [];
      score = 0;
      phase = "playing";
      // Start the animation loop.
      startLoop();
    }

    // === LOOP/UPDATE ==============================================================
    let loopId = null;
    let lastTime = performance.now();

    function startLoop() {
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
      function tick(now) {
        try {
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;
          update(dt);
          render();
        } catch (e) {
          console.error("loop error", e);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }

    function update(dt) {
      // Placeholder for future logic.
    }

    // === RENDER ==============================================================
    function render() {
      // Clear canvas
      ctx.fillStyle = "#05050c";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      // Draw player (if exists)
      if (player) {
        ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
        ctx.beginPath();
        ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
        ctx.fill();
      }
      // Draw score HUD
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.textAlign = "left";
      ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
      // Game over overlay
      if (phase === "gameover") {
        ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(5,5,12,0.65)";
        ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
        ctx.textAlign = "center";
        ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
        ctx.fillText("Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
        ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
        ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
        ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
        ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
      }
    }

    // === BOOT ==============================================================
    reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>

## Concept
The player taps to unleash an expanding shockwave that pushes nearby obstacles outward, briefly clearing a corridor for the dot to advance. The satisfaction comes from instantly reshaping the field and timing bursts to forge a path.

## Player
The player controls a small glowing dot that drifts forward. Visually it is a tiny neon sphere. Its verbs are:

## Hazards / Obstacles
- **Sliding blocks** that glide horizontally or vertically, colliding with the dot if not displaced.
- **Rotating spinners** that spin in place and extend outward, threatening the dot’s path.
- **Static walls** that sit in the arena; when struck by a shockwave they are pushed temporarily before resettling.
- **Bursting mines** that remain dormant until the shockwave reaches them, then explode outward, forcing the player to time bursts carefully.

All obstacles are pushed outward by the shockwave for a brief moment, then resume their original motion.

prompt (3,641 chars)
Read CONVENTIONS.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is **game design only**. Do NOT write any HTML or JavaScript code. Do NOT specify pixel coordinates, sprite sizes, or pixels-per-second speeds. The next stage will translate your design into an implementation plan; the stage after that will write the code. Your job here is to describe what the game IS, not how it's built.

Title: Force Burst
Concept: Tap creates an expanding shockwave that pushes nearby obstacles outward for a brief moment, clearing a temporary corridor for the dot to advance.

**You must produce one output file: `DESIGN.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("DESIGN.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the design as inline chat text.** The design must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"DESIGN.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward.

`DESIGN.md` MUST contain these sections (use these exact headers; the validator checks for them):

## Concept
One or two sentences in your own words. What does the player do, and why is it satisfying?

## Player
Describe what the player controls — the avatar, ship, cursor, paddle, whatever it is. Visually, what is it (one sentence)? List its verbs (move, jump, shoot, place, type). Keep it abstract — no coordinates, no speeds.

## Core loop
What happens every 1–3 seconds of normal play. Be specific about the moment-to-moment experience: what's the player reading, what are they choosing between, what makes them lean forward.

## Hazards / Obstacles
What stands in the player's way. Describe each kind by behavior and threat — "fast horizontal sweepers that telegraph their path", "stationary blocks that explode when shot". Concepts and behaviors, not numbers.

## Score
What discrete events increase the score. Examples: "survived a wave", "collected a pickup", "killed an enemy at long range". Avoid score = elapsed time as the only mechanic.

## Win and lose
When does the player win? When do they lose? What happens on screen the moment it ends.

## Feel
The vibe. Pace (frantic / steady / meditative). Aesthetic direction (neon / pastel / monochrome / retro CRT). Sound character (chiptune / arcade beeps / silent). One short paragraph.

## Why it's fun
One sentence. The single moment that would make a player smile, curse, or shout. What is this game's hook?

Keep DESIGN.md focused on player experience. The next phase (tasks) will translate this into an implementation plan with concrete numbers.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `DESIGN.md` as the path. The player can lose\n105: \n106: There must be a collision check (or other end condition) that fires `gameOver` and stops the active gameplay loop. \"Game runs forever, no death\" is broken. prompt (15,872 chars)
## Reference example: "asteroids"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Asteroids",
  "description": "Rotate, thrust, and shoot. Big rocks split into small ones. Get hit and you're gone.",
  "controls": [
    "Left/Right (or A/D) to rotate",
    "Up (or W) to thrust",
    "Space to fire",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "shooter", "physics"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Asteroids</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #05050c; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Asteroids</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Rotate, thrust, and shoot. Big rocks split into small ones. Get hit and you're gone.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Left/Right (or A/D) to rotate</li>
      <li>Up (or W) to thrust</li>
      <li>Space to fire</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const W = 800, H = 600;
  const SHIP_RADIUS = 12;
  const SHIP_THRUST = 280;       // px/sec^2
  const SHIP_FRICTION = 0.6;     // velocity decay per second (1 = none)
  const SHIP_TURN = Math.PI * 1.8; // rad/sec
  const BULLET_SPEED = 520;      // px/sec
  const BULLET_LIFE = 1.0;       // sec
  const FIRE_COOLDOWN = 0.20;    // sec between shots
  const ASTEROID_BIG_R = 38;
  const ASTEROID_MID_R = 22;
  const ASTEROID_LIL_R = 12;
  const ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE = [40, 110];
  const SAFE_RADIUS_AT_SPAWN = 160; // no asteroid spawns inside this disk

  const SCORE_BIG = 20, SCORE_MID = 50, SCORE_LIL = 100;

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let ship;        // { x, y, vx, vy, angle, alive }
  let bullets;     // [{ x, y, vx, vy, life }]
  let asteroids;   // [{ x, y, vx, vy, r, shape: number[] }]
  let score;
  let highScore = 0;
  let phase;       // "playing" | "gameover"
  let fireCooldown;
  let keys;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    ship = { x: W / 2, y: H / 2, vx: 0, vy: 0, angle: -Math.PI / 2, alive: true };
    bullets = [];
    asteroids = [];
    spawnWave(5);
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    fireCooldown = 0;
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  function spawnWave(n) {
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) spawnAsteroid(ASTEROID_BIG_R);
  }

  function spawnAsteroid(r) {
    let x, y, tries = 0;
    do {
      x = Math.random() * W;
      y = Math.random() * H;
      tries++;
    } while (Math.hypot(x - ship.x, y - ship.y) < SAFE_RADIUS_AT_SPAWN && tries < 30);
    const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
    const speed = ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0] + Math.random() * (ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[1] - ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0]);
    asteroids.push({
      x, y,
      vx: Math.cos(angle) * speed,
      vy: Math.sin(angle) * speed,
      r,
      shape: makeAsteroidShape(),
    });
  }

  function makeAsteroidShape() {
    // Per-vertex radius multipliers, 8-12 vertices for a knobbly silhouette.
    const n = 8 + Math.floor(Math.random() * 5);
    const out = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) out.push(0.75 + Math.random() * 0.45);
    return out;
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Ship.
    if (ship.alive) {
      const turnIn = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
                   - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
      ship.angle += turnIn * SHIP_TURN * dt;

      const thrusting = keys.has("ArrowUp") || keys.has("w") || keys.has("W");
      if (thrusting) {
        ship.vx += Math.cos(ship.angle) * SHIP_THRUST * dt;
        ship.vy += Math.sin(ship.angle) * SHIP_THRUST * dt;
      }
      // Mild friction so the ship doesn't drift forever.
      const decay = Math.pow(SHIP_FRICTION, dt);
      ship.vx *= decay;
      ship.vy *= decay;
      ship.x = wrap(ship.x + ship.vx * dt, W);
      ship.y = wrap(ship.y + ship.vy * dt, H);

      fireCooldown -= dt;
      if ((keys.has(" ") || keys.has("Space")) && fireCooldown <= 0) {
        fire();
        fireCooldown = FIRE_COOLDOWN;
      }
    }

    // Bullets.
    for (const b of bullets) {
      b.life -= dt;
      b.x = wrap(b.x + b.vx * dt, W);
      b.y = wrap(b.y + b.vy * dt, H);
    }
    bullets = bullets.filter((b) => b.life > 0);

    // Asteroids.
    for (const a of asteroids) {
      a.x = wrap(a.x + a.vx * dt, W);
      a.y = wrap(a.y + a.vy * dt, H);
    }

    // Bullet-asteroid collisions.
    for (let i = asteroids.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
      const a = asteroids[i];
      let hit = false;
      for (let j = bullets.length - 1; j >= 0; j--) {
        const b = bullets[j];
        if (Math.hypot(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y) < a.r) {
          bullets.splice(j, 1);
          hit = true;
          break;
        }
      }
      if (hit) splitAsteroid(i);
    }

    // Ship-asteroid collisions.
    if (ship.alive) {
      for (const a of asteroids) {
        if (Math.hypot(a.x - ship.x, a.y - ship.y) < a.r + SHIP_RADIUS * 0.6) {
          ship.alive = false;
          phase = "gameover";
          if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
          beep(110, 0.3);
          break;
        }
      }
    }

    // Wave clear -> spawn next.
    if (asteroids.length === 0 && phase === "playing") {
      spawnWave(5);
    }
  }

  function fire() {
    bullets.push({
      x: ship.x + Math.cos(ship.angle) * SHIP_RADIUS,
      y: ship.y + Math.sin(ship.angle) * SHIP_RADIUS,
      vx: ship.vx + Math.cos(ship.angle) * BULLET_SPEED,
      vy: ship.vy + Math.sin(ship.angle) * BULLET_SPEED,
      life: BULLET_LIFE,
    });
    beep(820, 0.04);
  }

  function splitAsteroid(idx) {
    const a = asteroids[idx];
    asteroids.splice(idx, 1);
    if (a.r === ASTEROID_BIG_R) {
      score += SCORE_BIG;
      for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) addChild(a, ASTEROID_MID_R);
      beep(440, 0.06);
    } else if (a.r === ASTEROID_MID_R) {
      score += SCORE_MID;
      for (let i = 0; i < 2; i++) addChild(a, ASTEROID_LIL_R);
      beep(560, 0.05);
    } else {
      score += SCORE_LIL;
      beep(700, 0.05);
    }
  }

  function addChild(parent, r) {
    const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
    const speed = ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0] + Math.random() * (ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[1] - ASTEROID_SPEED_RANGE[0]);
    asteroids.push({
      x: parent.x, y: parent.y,
      vx: Math.cos(angle) * speed,
      vy: Math.sin(angle) * speed,
      r,
      shape: makeAsteroidShape(),
    });
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#05050c";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);

    // Asteroids.
    ctx.strokeStyle = "#a5a5b8";
    ctx.lineWidth = 1.5;
    for (const a of asteroids) drawAsteroid(a);

    // Bullets.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffd23f";
    for (const b of bullets) {
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.arc(b.x, b.y, 2.4, 0, Math.PI * 2);
      ctx.fill();
    }

    // Ship.
    if (ship.alive) drawShip();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Best ${highScore}`, W - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "gameover") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(5,5,12,0.65)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, W, H);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText("Game Over", W / 2, H / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, W / 2, H / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", W / 2, H / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  function drawShip() {
    ctx.save();
    ctx.translate(ship.x, ship.y);
    ctx.rotate(ship.angle);
    ctx.strokeStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.lineWidth = 2;
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.moveTo(SHIP_RADIUS, 0);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.9, SHIP_RADIUS * 0.7);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.5, 0);
    ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.9, -SHIP_RADIUS * 0.7);
    ctx.closePath();
    ctx.stroke();
    if (keys && (keys.has("ArrowUp") || keys.has("w") || keys.has("W"))) {
      ctx.strokeStyle = "#ffd23f";
      ctx.beginPath();
      ctx.moveTo(-SHIP_RADIUS * 0.5, 0);
      ctx.lineTo(-SHIP_RADIUS - 6 - Math.random() * 4, 0);
      ctx.stroke();
    }
    ctx.restore();
  }

  function drawAsteroid(a) {
    ctx.beginPath();
    for (let i = 0; i < a.shape.length; i++) {
      const t = (i / a.shape.length) * Math.PI * 2;
      const r = a.r * a.shape[i];
      const x = a.x + Math.cos(t) * r;
      const y = a.y + Math.sin(t) * r;
      if (i === 0) ctx.moveTo(x, y); else ctx.lineTo(x, y);
    }
    ctx.closePath();
    ctx.stroke();
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
    if (ev.key === " ") ev.preventDefault();
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "triangle";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.04, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase,                                  // "playing" | "gameover"
      score,
      player: {
        x: ship.x,
        y: ship.y,
        // wrap() keeps coords in [0, W) and [0, H), so visible is always true
        // — but assert it explicitly so the contract is honest.
        visible: ship.x >= 0 && ship.x < W && ship.y >= 0 && ship.y < H,
      },
      objective: "Shoot the asteroids before they hit your ship. Big rocks split into smaller ones.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function wrap(v, max) {
    if (v < 0) return v + max;
    if (v >= max) return v - max;
    return v;
  }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

 const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

 let player = {
   x: canvas.width / 2,
   y: canvas.height / 2,
   radius: 10,
   visible: true,
 };

 let hazards = [];

 let score = 0;
 let phase = "menu";

 const PLAYER_SPEED = 200; // px per second

 let keys = new Set();
 let loopId = null;
 let lastTime = performance.now();

 // === RESET ==============================================================
 function reset() {
   // Reset game state
   player.x = canvas.width / 2;
   player.y = canvas.height / 2;
   player.visible = true;
   hazards = [];
   score = 0;
   phase = "playing";
   keys.clear();
   startLoop();
 }

 // === LOOP/UPDATE ==============================================================
 function startLoop() {
   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
   function tick(now) {
     try {
       const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
       lastTime = now;
       update(dt);
       render();
     } catch (e) {
       console.error("loop error", e);
     }
     loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
   }
   lastTime = performance.now();
   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
 }

 function update(dt) {
   if (phase !== "playing") return;
   let dx = 0, dy = 0;
   if (keys.has("ArrowLeft") || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A")) dx -= 1;
   if (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D")) dx += 1;
   if (keys.has("ArrowUp") || keys.has("w") || keys.has("W")) dy -= 1;
   if (keys.has("ArrowDown") || keys.has("s") || keys.has("S")) dy += 1;
   // Normalize diagonal movement (optional)
   if (dx !== 0 && dy !== 0) {
     const invSqrt2 = 1 / Math.sqrt(2);
     dx *= invSqrt2;
     dy *= invSqrt2;
   }
   player.x += dx * PLAYER_SPEED * dt;
   player.y += dy * PLAYER_SPEED * dt;
   // Keep player within bounds
   if (player.x < 0) player.x = 0;
   if (player.x > canvas.width) player.x = canvas.width;
   if (player.y < 0) player.y = 0;
   if (player.y > canvas.height) player.y = canvas.height;
 }

 // === RENDER ==============================================================
 function render() {
   // Clear canvas
   ctx.fillStyle = "#05050c";
   ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
   // Draw player
   ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
   ctx.beginPath();
   ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
   ctx.fill();
 }

 // === INPUT ==============================================================
 document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
   if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") {
     reset();
     return;
   }
   keys.add(ev.key);
   if (ev.key === " ") ev.preventDefault();
 });
 document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => {
   keys.delete(ev.key);
 });
 restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

 // === AUDIO (lazy) ===========================================================
 // Placeholder for later

 // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
 // Placeholder for later

 // === HELPERS ==============================================================
 // Placeholder for later

 // === BOOT ==============================================================
 reset();
</script>
```

Potential newString:

```
    function render() {
      // Clear canvas
      ctx.fillStyle = "#05050c";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      // Draw player (if alive)
      if (player && player.visible !== false) {
        ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
        ctx.beginPath();
        ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, player.radius, 0, Math.PI * 2);
        ctx.fill();
      }
      // Draw score HUD
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.textAlign = "left";
      ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
      // Game over overlay
      if (phase === "gameover") {
        ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(5,5,12,0.65)";
        ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
        ctx.textAlign = "center";
        ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
        ctx.fillText("Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
        ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
        ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
        ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
        ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
        ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
      }
    }
```

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Force Burst
Concept: Tap creates an expanding shockwave that pushes nearby obstacles outward for a brief moment, clearing a temporary corridor for the dot to advance.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path. If you only described the task list in chat without invoking the tool, the file will not exist on disk and validation will fail. After the file is written, stop. Do not begin implementation.

Per-frame constants (`x += 5` every frame) make games run 2.4× faster on a 144Hz display.\n123: \n124: ```js\n125: let lastTime = performance.now();\n126: function loop(now) {\n127:   const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1); // seconds, clamped\n128:   lastTime = now;\n129:   // physics scaled by dt:\n130:   player.x += player.vx * dt;\n131:   enemy.y  += enemy.vy  * dt;\n132:   spawnTimer -= dt;\n133:   // ...\n134:   requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n135: }\n136: requestAnimationFrame(loop);\n137: ```\n138: \n139: ### 6. Restart fully resets AND cancels the prior loop\n140: \n141: Both R and the Restart button must call the same reset function. That function MUST `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` the previous loop before scheduling a new one — otherwise loops stack on each restart and the game slows to a crawl over time.\n142: \n143: ```js\n144: let loopId = null;\n145: function startLoop() {\n146:   if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);\n147:   function tick(now) { /* ... */ loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick); }\n148:   loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n149: }\n150: ```\n151: \n152: ### 7. Game-over overlay shows score and restart instructions\n153: \n154: When the player dies, draw on the canvas: \"Game Over\", final score, current high score (if you persist one), and \"Press R or click Restart\". Do not rely on the HTML restart button alone — players need to know they can restart.\n155: \n156: ## Observable State Contract — required for playtest\n157: \n158: In addition to the rules above, your game **must** expose its current state on `window.__game` so the automated playtest can observe what the player would observe. A game that does not expose this object is rejected.\n159: \n160: ```js\n161: window.__game = {\n162:   state: \"menu\",        // \"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\" — exact strings, no others\n163:   score: 0,             // current score, number\n164:   player: {\n165:     x: 400,             // player x in canvas coordinates (0 = left)\n166:     y: 300,             // player y in canvas coordinates (0 = top)\n167:     visible: true,      // true if the player is currently rendered inside the canvas viewport\n168:   },\n169:   objective: \"Dodge falling neon blocks. Survive as long as possible.\", // 10-200 chars, one sentence\n170: };\n171: ```\n172: \n173: Rules:\n174: - The object must exist from the very first frame, before any input.\n175: - Update `state`, `score`, `player.x`, `player.y`, and `player.visible` whenever they change. The simplest approach is to update them at the end of your render/update step each frame.\n176: - `state` must only ever be one of the three exact strings. No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. No external URLs in `<script src=>`, `<link href=>`, `<img src=>` (regex scan).\n190: 5. Headless Chromium loads the page; no `console.error` or `pageerror` events in 5 seconds.\n191: 6. Canvas has non-blank pixels after 3 seconds (game is actually drawing something).\n192: 7. **Observable contract is present**: `window.__game` exists with the exact shape above, from the first frame.\n193: 8. **Playtest invariants** hold during ~8 seconds of synthetic key input (arrow keys, WASD, space):\n194:    - `state` only takes the values `\"menu\" | \"playing\" | \"gameover\"`.\n195:    - `player.visible` is true for ≥95% of polled frames.\n196:    - Either `score` changes at least once during play, or `state` reaches `\"gameover\"`.
